{'role': 'user', 'content': [{'type': 'text', 'text': '<file name="D:\\dev\\ab-tasks\\_briefs\\anyio\\brief.md">\n# Task: fix a reported bug in `anyio`\r\n\r\n## Bug report (verbatim from the project\'s issue tracker)\r\n**CapacityLimiter can over-grant tokens (`borrowed_tokens > total_tokens`, `available_tokens` goes negative)**\r\n\r\n### Things to check first\r\n\r\n- [x] I have searched the existing issues and didn\'t find my bug already reported there\r\n\r\n- [x] I have checked that my bug is still present in the latest release\r\n\r\n\r\n### AnyIO version\r\n\r\n4.13.0\r\n\r\n### Python version\r\n\r\n3.12\r\n\r\n### What happened?\r\n\r\nOn the asyncio backend, a non-blocking acquire can succeed *while the just-freed token is already reserved for a woken waiter*, leaving the limiter with more borrowers than it has tokens. The state becomes:\r\n\r\n```\r\nborrowed_tokens  = 2\r\ntotal_tokens     = 1\r\navailable_tokens = -1      # negative\r\nborrowers        = (\'B\', \'X\')\r\n```\r\n\r\nTwo tasks then hold a single-token limiter concurrently — the exact condition the limiter exists to prevent — and `available_tokens` is negative.\r\n\r\nThis violates the documented contract of `acquire_nowait` / `acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait`:\r\n\r\n> :raises ~anyio.WouldBlock: if there are no tokens available for borrowing\r\n\r\nIn the window below there is no token available (the freed one is earmarked for the woken waiter), yet the nowait acquire succeeds instead of raising `WouldBlock`.\r\n\r\n### How can we reproduce the bug?\r\n\r\nJust anyio, no test framework: `python repro.py` prints the corrupt state and raises `AssertionError`:\r\n\r\n```python\r\nimport anyio\r\nfrom anyio import CapacityLimiter, WouldBlock\r\n\r\nasync def main():\r\n    lim = CapacityLimiter(1)                       # capacity: exactly ONE token\r\n    lim.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait("A")           # A borrows the only token\r\n\r\n    async with anyio.create_task_group() as tg:\r\n        async def b():\r\n            await lim.acquire_on_behalf_of("B")    # B waits for the token\r\n        tg.start_soon(b)\r\n\r\n        while lim.statistics().tasks_waiting != 1:  # wait until B is parked\r\n            await anyio.sleep(0)\r\n\r\n        lim.release_on_behalf_of("A")              # frees the token, notifies B\r\n        # B has been notified but has NOT resumed yet, so it is not in\r\n        # _borrowers and the wait queue is now empty. A non-blocking acquire\r\n        # in this window slips through:\r\n        try:\r\n            lim.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait("X")  # expected: WouldBlock\r\n            print("X acquired without waiting (should have raised WouldBlock)")\r\n        except WouldBlock:\r\n            print("X correctly blocked")\r\n        # leaving the task group lets B resume and add itself to _borrowers\r\n\r\n    print(f"borrowed_tokens  = {lim.borrowed_tokens}  (total_tokens = {lim.total_tokens})")\r\n    print(f"available_tokens = {lim.available_tokens}")\r\n    print(f"borrowers        = {lim.statistics().borrowers}")\r\n    assert lim.borrowed_tokens <= lim.total_tokens, "borrowed_tokens > total_tokens"\r\n\r\nanyio.run(main)\r\n```\r\n\r\nOutput:\r\n\r\n```\r\nX acquired without waiting (should have raised WouldBlock)\r\nborrowed_tokens  = 2  (total_tokens = 1)\r\navailable_tokens = -1\r\nborrowers        = (\'B\', \'X\')\r\nAssertionError: borrowed_tokens > total_tokens\r\n```\r\n### Cause\r\n\r\nIn `anyio/_backends/_asyncio.py`:\r\n\r\n- `release_on_behalf_of` removes the borrower then calls `_notify_next_waiter()`, which **pops** the next waiter out of `_wait_queue` and does `event.set()`.\r\n- The woken waiter, in `acquire_on_behalf_of`, only adds itself to `_borrowers` **after** `await event.wait()` returns:\r\n\r\n  ```python\r\n  await event.wait()\r\n  ...\r\n  self._borrowers.add(borrower)   # runs only once the waiter resumes\r\n  ```\r\n\r\n- Between the `event.set()` and the waiter resuming, `_wait_queue` is empty and the waiter is not yet in `_borrowers`. So the nowait guard\r\n\r\n  ```python\r\n  if self._wait_queue or len(self._borrowers) >= self._total_tokens:\r\n      raise WouldBlock\r\n  ```\r\n\r\n  evaluates to `False` (empty queue, `0 < 1`) and admits a second borrower for the one token.\r\n\r\n### Why `Semaphore` is not affected\r\n\r\nThe sibling `Semaphore` is immune under the identical scenario, a nowait acquire issued in the same window correctly raises `WouldBlock`, because its `release()` hands the freed slot to the woken waiter rather than leaving it generally available. `CapacityLimiter` is the one primitive whose freed-but-reserved token is briefly visible as "available" to a synchronous acquirer.\r\n\r\nA fix would close that window, e.g. by reserving the token for the woken waiter at notify time (add the borrower to `_borrowers` when `_notify_next_waiter` sets its event, rather than after it resumes), mirroring the Semaphore/Lock hand-off. The nowait guard would then correctly see no free token.\r\n\r\n## Standing instructions (identical for every run)\r\nYou are working in the repository at the current working directory. Work like a careful open-source contributor:\r\n1. Reproduce the bug first (write a minimal failing test or script) before changing code.\r\n2. Find the root cause; fix the cause, not the symptom. Keep the change minimal and in the project\'s style.\r\n3. Add or update a regression test that fails before your fix and passes after.\r\n4. Run the project\'s test suite and make it green. Do not modify or delete unrelated tests.\r\n5. When done, summarize: root cause, the files you changed, how you verified it.\r\nDo not ask me for permission between steps; proceed until the task is complete, then report.\r\n\r\nTest command for this repo: `.venv/Scripts/python -m pytest tests/test_synchronization.py -q`\r\n\n</file>\n'}], 'attribution': 'user', 'timestamp': 1787264584057}

Let\'s analyze the bug:\n\n`release_on_behalf_of`:\n```python\ndef release_on_behalf_of(self, borrower: object) -> None:\n    try:\n        self._borrowers.remove(borrower)\n    except KeyError:\n        raise RuntimeError(...) from None\n    self._notify_next_waiter()\n```\n\n`_notify_next_waiter`:\n```python\ndef _notify_next_waiter(self) -> None:\n    """Notify the next task in line if this limiter has free capacity now."""\n    if self._wait_queue and len(self._borrowers) < self._total_tokens:\n        event = self._wait_queue.popitem(last=False)[1]\n        event.set()\n```\n\n`acquire_on_behalf_of`:\n```python\nasync def acquire_on_behalf_of(self, borrower: object) -> None:\n    await AsyncIOBackend.checkpoint_if_cancelled()\n    try:\n        self.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait(borrower)\n    except WouldBlock:\n        event = asyncio.Event()\n        self._wait_queue[borrower] = event\n        try:\n            await event.wait()\n        except BaseException:\n            self._wait_queue.pop(borrower, None)\n            if event.is_set():\n                self._notify_next_waiter()\n            raise\n\n        self._borrowers.add(borrower)\n    else:\n        try:\n            await AsyncIOBackend.cancel_shielded_checkpoint()\n        except BaseException:\n            self.release()\n            raise\n```\n\nRace: `release_on_behalf_of` removes A from `_borrowers` (borrowed count goes to 0) and calls `_notify_next_waiter`, which pops B\'s event and sets it. At this point:\n- `_borrowers` = {} (length 0)\n- `_wait_queue` = {} (B was popped)\n- B has not yet been added to `_borrowers`\n\nSo `acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait("X")` in the same synchronous window:\n- `self._wait_queue` is empty\n- `len(self._borrowers) = 0 < self._total_tokens = 1`\n- Therefore X is added to `_borrowers` → over-grant\n\nAfter B resumes:\n- B executes `self._borrowers.add(borrower)` → `_borrowers` = {B, X} → borrowed = 2 > total = 1 → `available_tokens` = 1 - 2 = -1.\n\nThe fix proposed in the issue: reserve the token for the woken waiter at notify time. That is, when `_notify_next_waiter` sets the event, add the borrower to `_borrowers`. Then in `acquire_on_behalf_of`, the waiter should not re-add (since it\'s already added).\n\nBut wait — there\'s a subtlety. If the waiter is cancelled after the event is set but before it resumes, the `except BaseException` branch pops it from `_wait_queue` (already popped) and calls `self._notify_next_waiter()` if `event.is_set()`. If we add the borrower to `_borrowers` at notify time, we need to handle the cancellation path: remove the borrower from `_borrowers` and re-notify the next waiter.\n\nLet me think about the correct design.\n\nActually, let me reconsider. The cleanest fix that mirrors what upstream anyio actually did. Let me recall: this is a real anyio bug. Let me think about how upstream fixed it.\n\nActually, this is a known anyio bug. Let me think about what the actual upstream fix was. The issue is about `CapacityLimiter` over-granting.\n\nLet me think carefully about the semantics. The core problem is that between "popping the waiter from the queue + setting the event" and "the waiter actually resuming and adding itself to `_borrowers`", the token is in a limbo state where it\'s neither held by anyone (not in `_borrowers`) nor reserved (not in `_wait_queue`). So `acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait` sees a free token.\n\nThe fix: when we notify the next waiter (pop from queue, set event), we should immediately transfer the token — i.e., add the borrower to `_borrowers`. This makes the "reserved" state visible: the waiter is in `_borrowers` (holding the token), so `acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait` correctly sees `len(self._borrowers) >= self._total_tokens` and raises `WouldBlock`.\n\nBut then, if the woken waiter is cancelled before resuming (in the window between event.set() and event.wait() returning), we must remove it from `_borrowers` and hand off to the next waiter.\n\nWait, actually there\'s a problem with cancellation. But the current code checks `if event.is_set(): self._notify_next_waiter()`. {'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'Bug confirmed. Now let me analyze the root cause and design the fix.\n\nThe core issue: `_notify_next_waiter` pops the waiter from `_wait_queue` and sets its event, but the waiter only adds itself to `_borrowers` after resuming. During the window between `event.set()` and the waiter resuming:\n- `_wait_queue` is empty\n- The waiter is not in `_borrowers`\n- So `acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait` sees a free token and admits a second borrower.\n\nThe fix: reserve the token for the woken waiter at notify time. That is, add the borrower to `_borrowers` when `_notify_next_waiter` sets its event.\n\nBut there are two paths that set events:\n1. `_notify_next_waiter` (called from `release_on_behalf_of`, the cancellation path, and `acquire`\'s `cancel_shielded_checkpoint`? no)\n2. The `total_tokens` setter\n\nLet me handle both consistently.\n\nLet me look at the whole flow again:\n\n```python\ndef _notify_next_waiter(self) -> None:\n    """Notify the next task in line if this limiter has free capacity now."""\n    if self._wait_queue and len(self._borrowers) < self._total_tokens:\n        event = self._wait_queue.popitem(last=False)[1]\n        event.set()\n```\n\nI\'ll change this to:\n```python\ndef _notify_next_waiter(self) -> None:\n    """Notify the next task in line if this limiter has free capacity now."""\n    if self._wait_queue and len(self._borrowers) < self._total_tokens:\n        borrower, event = self._wait_queue.popitem(last=False)\n        self._borrowers.add(borrower)  # hand the token to the waiter now\n        event.set()\n```\n\nAnd the `total_tokens` setter:\n```python\n@total_tokens.setter\ndef total_tokens(self, value: float) -> None:\n    if not isinstance(value, int) and not math.isinf(value):\n        raise TypeError("total_tokens must be an int or math.inf")\n\n    if value < 0:\n        raise ValueError("total_tokens must be >= 0")\n\n    waiters_to_notify = max(value - self._total_tokens, 0)\n    self._total_tokens = value\n\n    # Notify waiting tasks that they have acquired the limiter\n    while self._wait_queue and waiters_to_notify:\n        event = self._wait_queue.popitem(last=False)[1]\n        event.set()\n        waiters_to_notify -= 1\n```\n\nHmm, the setter notifies `waiters_to_notify` waiters. But if we add each to `_borrowers`, we need to be careful: we should only notify up to the number of available tokens. Actually the current code uses `waiters_to_notify = max(value - self._total_tokens, 0)`, which is the increase in capacity. But there might already be borrowers. Let me think.\n\nActually, let me reconsider. When total_tokens increases from old to new, the number of newly available tokens is (new - old). But some of the existing capacity may already be borrowed. The number of waiters we can satisfy is `min(len(_wait_queue), new - len(_borrowers))`.\n\nWait, but the current code does `waiters_to_notify = max(value - self._total_tokens, 0)`, which is `new - old`, not `new - len(_borrowers)`. Hmm, that seems slightly off too, but let me not change behavior beyond the fix. Actually, wait, let me reconsider.\n\nActually, hmm. Let me reconsider. If old=1, new=3, and there\'s 1 borrower (so old available = 0, new available = 2). The code notifies `new - old = 2` waiters. That\'s correct if there was 1 borrower (2 newly available). But if there were 0 borrowers (1 available before), new available = 3, so it should notify 3, but the code only notifies 2. Hmm, that\'s a pre-existing bug but not what I\'m fixing. Actually wait — if there were 0 borrowers and 1 available token, would there be waiters in the queue? No, because a nowait acquire would succeed when available > 0 and no waiters. Wait, but waiters only enter the queue when a nowait acquire fails (WouldBlock). A nowait acquire fails if `self._wait_queue or len(self._borrowers) >= self._total_tokens`. So a waiter enters the queue only when `len(_borrowers) >= total_tokens` (or there\'s already a queue). So when waiters exist, `_borrowers` is full (or queue non-empty). So `new - old` = newly available tokens, and since borrowers was full at old capacity, borrowers is still full... no wait.\n\nLet me not go down this rabbit hole. Let me focus on the specific bug and make a minimal, correct fix. But I do need to make the `total_tokens` setter consistent with my change (adding to `_borrowers` on notify), otherwise a waiter woken by the setter would find itself already... no wait. The key insight from the issue:\n\n> A fix would close that window, e.g. by reserving the token for the woken waiter at notify time (add the borrower to `_borrowers` when `_notify_next_waiter` sets its event, rather than after it resumes), mirroring the Semaphore/Lock hand-off.\n\nSo my approach matches the issue\'s suggested fix. Let me implement it.\n\nNow, an important consideration: should I keep `self._borrowers.add(borrower)` at line 2131 (after event.wait() returns)? \n\nWith my fix, the borrower is ALREADY added to `_borrowers` when its event is set (by `_notify_next_waiter` or the setter). So line 2131 `self._borrowers.add(borrower)` is redundant. But is it harmful? No, it\'s a set, idempotent. But it\'s now misleading. \n\nHowever, wait — there\'s a subtle issue. What if I DON\'T remove it, and rely on it? Then the borrower is added to `_borrowers` at notify time (closing the window) AND re-added at resume (no-op). That\'s fine and keeps line 2131 as a "safety" in case there\'s a path where event is set but borrower not added. But as established, both paths that set the event add the borrower, so it\'s always redundant.\n\nFor minimal change and to keep the invariant clear, I think the cleanest is:\n- `_notify_next_waiter`: add borrower to `_borrowers` when setting event.\n- setter: add borrower to `_borrowers` when setting event.\n- `acquire_on_behalf_of` normal path: remove the now-redundant `self._borrowers.add(borrower)` (since it\'s done at notify time). But actually, keeping it is harmless and defensive.\n\nHmm, let me think about which is more "in the project\'s style" and minimal. \n\nActually, I realize there might be an even cleaner and safer approach that avoids the redundant add entirely and keeps the cancellation logic clean. Let me reconsider.\n\nThe concern with removing line 2131: If there\'s any path where the event is set but the borrower is NOT in `_borrowers`, then the borrower would proceed holding a token without being tracked → undercount of borrowers. But we\'ve established both setting paths add to `_borrowers`. So it\'s safe to remove.\n\nBut actually, I want to be defensive here. Let me reconsider: is it really guaranteed that the event is ONLY set through `_notify_next_waiter` and the setter? The event object is created locally in `acquire_on_behalf_of` and stored in `_wait_queue[borrower]`. The only code that can access it is through `_wait_queue` (popitem in `_notify_next_waiter` and setter). So yes, only those two. Let me think.\n\nIn asyncio, when you `await event.wait()`:\n- If the event is already set when the task runs, `event.wait()` returns immediately (no suspension), so the task proceeds to the normal path. Any pending cancellation would be delivered at the NEXT suspension/checkpoint.\n- If the event is not set, the task suspends. If cancelled while suspended, `event.wait()` raises CancelledError → except path.\n\nSo the "cancelled after event set" case (Case 2) happens when: the event was set while the task was suspended, and the cancellation is delivered. In asyncio, when both a cancellation and an event.set() are pending, the order matters. But regardless, if `event.wait()` raises CancelledError AND `event.is_set()` is True, we handle it in Case 2. If `event.wait()` returns normally (event set wins), the task is in the normal path and holds the token; any subsequent cancellation is a separate matter (handled at the next checkpoint, outside our concern here — actually, it would be handled by the task\'s own cancel scope).\n\nWait, actually, there\'s a real subtlety here that I should get right. In asyncio, a different task (B) can only run if the current task yields (awaits something that suspends). `release_on_behalf_of` and `acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait` are both synchronous (no await). {'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'The user asks: does my fix also hold when `total_tokens` is raised while tasks are waiting? Add a test for that path.\n\nLet me think about this carefully. The `total_tokens` setter path is one of the two places I fixed (the one that pops+sets a waiter\'s event). Let me reason about whether the fix holds there.\n\nThe setter code (after my fix):\n```python\n@total_tokens.setter\ndef total_tokens(self, value: float) -> None:\n    if not isinstance(value, int) and not math.isinf(value):\n        raise TypeError("total_tokens must be an int or math.inf")\n\n    if value < 0:\n        raise ValueError("total_tokens must be >= 0")\n\n    waiters_to_notify = max(value - self._total_tokens, 0)\n    self._total_tokens = value\n\n    # Notify waiting tasks that they have acquired the limiter\n    while self._wait_queue and waiters_to_notify:\n        borrower, event = self._wait_queue.popitem(last=False)\n        # Hand the token to the waiter immediately (see _notify_next_waiter)\n        self._borrowers.add(borrower)\n        event.set()\n        waiters_to_notify -= 1\n```\n\nSo when `total_tokens` is raised while tasks are waiting:\n1. `waiters_to_notify = value - old_total`\n2. `self._total_tokens = value`\n3. For up to `waiters_to_notify` waiters (bounded by queue length): pop the waiter, add to `_borrowers`, set event.\n\nThe window I care about: after the setter pops a waiter and sets its event (and adds it to `_borrowers`), but before that waiter resumes, a nowait acquire should NOT over-grant.\n\nLet me think about the scenario:\n- `total_tokens = 1`.\n- A borrows the only token.\n- B, C wait (queue = [B, C]).\n- Now raise `total_tokens = 3`. \n  - `waiters_to_notify = 3 - 1 = 2`.\n  - Iteration 1: pop B, add B to `_borrowers` (now {A, B}), set B\'s event. `waiters_to_notify = 1`.\n  - Iteration 2: pop C, add C to `_borrowers` (now {A, B, C}), set C\'s event. `waiters_to_notify = 0`.\n- Now `_borrowers = {A, B, C}`, `total_tokens = 3`. `len(_borrowers) = 3 >= 3`.\n- A nowait acquire by X in the window (B and C not yet resumed): `self._wait_queue` is empty, `len(_borrowers) = 3 >= total_tokens = 3` → `WouldBlock`. Correct! No over-grant.\n\nSo the fix holds. Before the fix, the setter would pop B and C, set their events, but NOT add them to `_borrowers`. So `_borrowers = {A}`, `len = 1 < 3`.
